  [G9.10] Material replacement-related information omitted from the recommendation

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The report asserts (page 5, Existing Arrangements): (1) all four arrangements being switched/encashed contain no safeguarded benefits; (2) no employer contributions are lost on any transfer; (3) no exit penalties or transfer charges apply to any of the five existing arrangements ('No charges for transfer and/or switching'). The charges comparison (page 11) states the new Aviva plan is 0.24% less expensive on a like-for-like basis (£264 underperformance tolerance), but 4.24% more expensive including the adviser fee (£4,672 outperformance required). The Standard Life plan recommendation (page 8) states the existing plan is '0.08% more expensive than the recommended solution'. The charges table (page 11) shows the impact of the recommended alternative for Standard Life as +0.26% (an increase). The critical yield illustration (page 18) labels the mid growth rate as 2.95%; page 11 narrative refers to 2.94%.

    File:     [P11] Zurich policy: confirms no regular payments currently being made and no employer payments, consistent with no active employer contributions being lost. However, [P11] also discloses a potentially material charge: 'If this plan has capital units, there will be an exit charge applied if the fund is transferred. This exit charge is a standard term of the contract... The charge is capped at 1% from age 55.' [P12] Zurich charges table shows a 'Bid to offer spread' of 5% of all payments and an annual management charge of 3/4% per year. [P13] Zurich: confirms the transfer value/early retirement charge row states 'There are no deductions taken from the current value if benefits are transferred or taken. The initial set up charges have already been paid.' [P16] Standard Life: confirms no bid-offer spread, 100% allocation, and no platform charge; client can restart contributions at any time; is a defined contribution pension plan with no Protected Tax Free Cash beyond standard 25%. [P9] Standard Life: confirms current value of £3,002.02. [P15] Scottish Widows: confirms current value of £211.32 and illustrates charges reducing growth rate by 0.3% per year. [P20] Policy research file (category G): shows Market Value Reduction as 'No' for the relevant plans. [P22] Pension switch report (category G): confirms existing pension value at switch £110,185.60 and shows the effect of switching as negative across all growth scenarios (e.g. mid: -£12,000 / -9.4%; low: -£10,400 / -9.6%; high: -£15,000 / -10.0%), with critical yield at mid rate of 4.8%. [P1]-[P8],[P10] People's Pension: transaction history showing ongoing employer and employee contributions — this plan is stated to be retained, not switched. No provider documentation confirming absence of safeguarded benefits is present in the retrieved passages for any plan.

    Discrepancies:

      - The report asserts no exit penalties for the Zurich plan; [P11] discloses a potential exit charge on capital units ('If this plan has capital units, there will be an exit charge applied if the fund is transferred... capped at 1% from age 55'). The report makes no mention of this contingent charge or any analysis of whether capital units are held.

      - The report (page 8) states the Standard Life plan is '0.08% more expensive than the recommended solution' (i.e. switching saves 0.08%). The charges comparison table (page 11) shows the impact of the recommended alternative for Standard Life as '+0.26%' (a cost increase, not saving). These are directly contradictory.

      - The derived figures confirm that the EA5 (Standard Life) charge rows in the comparison table are computed on a fund value matching Zurich's current value (£103,438.89 / £103,438.46) rather than Standard Life's own value of £3,002.00, meaning the entire Standard Life cost comparison rests on an arithmetically wrong base figure.

      - No provider documentation confirming absence of safeguarded benefits is present in the evidence pack for any of the four switched/encashed plans (Aviva, Zurich, Standard Life, Scottish Widows). The report asserts this as a flat statement but no corroborating category E or G document confirms it.

      - The critical yield illustration labels the mid growth rate as '2.95%' (page 18 / [P22]) while page 11 narrative states '2.94%' — a minor inconsistency in the illustration inputs.

      - The effect-of-switching table ([P22]) shows negative outcomes at all three growth scenarios (low: -9.6%, mid: -9.4%, high: -10.0%) with a critical yield of 4.8% at mid rate. The report's rationale for switching rests substantially on cost savings and simplification, but the Standard Life cost comparison (the basis for one switch) is arithmetically unreliable (wrong fund value used), undermining the claimed benefit.

      - The Zurich policy ([P12]) discloses a 'Bid to offer spread' of 5% of all payments. No mention of this spread or its effect on the transfer value appears in the report's cost/benefit analysis.

    Several material concerns arise. First, the most significant: [P11] discloses that the Zurich plan may carry an exit charge on capital units at transfer, capped at 1% from age 55. The report flatly asserts 'No charges for transfer and/or switching' for all plans including Zurich. [P13] does contain a Zurich table row stating 'There are no deductions taken from the current value if benefits are transferred or taken. The initial set up charges have already been paid' — which appears to address the transfer charge position. However, [P11]'s capital units disclosure is a separate, conditional charge ('If this plan has capital units...') that applies to capital units specifically, and no document in the pack confirms whether capital units are held. The report does not address this at all. This is a material omission for a plan worth £103,439.24 (the largest ceding plan by value), where even a 1% charge capped exit penalty would represent over £1,000. Second, the Standard Life cost comparison is doubly unreliable: the page 8 narrative states a 0.08% saving, while the page 11 table shows +0.26% (a cost increase); and the derived figures confirm the table rows for EA5 are computed on Zurich's fund value (c.£103,438) rather than Standard Life's own £3,002. This means both the direction and the magnitude of the Standard Life cost claim are wrong. A switch justified in part on cost saving where the comparison table contradicts the narrative, and is itself computed on the wrong plan value, represents a high-severity failing. Third, no provider documentation corroborating the 'no safeguarded benefits' assertion is present in the pack for any plan. The severity guidance rates this as highest where no provider documentation confirms absence of safeguarded benefits. The People's Pension is retained (not switched), so the ongoing employer contributions visible in [P1]-[P8] are not lost — the guard applies: those contributions stop on leaving service and the plan is being retained. No vulnerability factors in the evidence appear to aggravate or alter these findings materially. The overall picture is: the largest ceding plan (Zurich, £103,439) has an unaddressed potential exit charge; the Standard Life cost comparison contains a direct internal contradiction and is computed on the wrong fund value; and no safeguarded benefits confirmation is evidenced for any plan.

CHK-010 — Vulnerability, Support Needs and Foreseeable Harm Overlay

Outcome: Potential Concern

 

  No trigger was recorded; this check applies to every case and was assessed anyway. Potential Concern across 5 requirement(s). 5 raised a concern (G10.1, G10.2, G10.3, G10.4, G10.5). The report records no vulnerability assessment outcome for either client, yet the file contains FG21/1 capability drivers: the client has stated 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares, EIS/VCTs, and pension planning; the policy research notes the client does not understand how his pensions work and finds multiple plans too much information to comprehend. No support needs, communication adjustments or comprehension measures are recorded anywhere in the report despite the presence of low-capability indicators in the file. No formal vulnerability assessment outcome is recorded anywhere in the case file (confirmed absent by extraction metadata for both /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome).

 

  [G10.1] Details of additional support and needs, including third-party support

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The canonical model holds no value at any of this group's paths. The extracted model confirms /vulnerability/perClient[]/supportNeeds[], /vulnerability/perClient[]/thirdPartySupport, /vulnerability/perClient[]/thirdPartySupport/authorityType, and /vulnerability/perClient[]/thirdPartySupport/relationship are all absent from the report. The extraction metadata confirms these paths are NotPresentInDocument for CHK-010. The suitability report does include a forward-looking recommendation that the client set up a Power of Attorney, but records no current third-party support, no current support needs, and no vulnerability assessment outcome for either client.

    File:     The fact find (category B) records no vulnerability indicators, no support needs, no third-party support arrangements, and no communication adjustments. The meeting notes record the meeting type as a Video Call - Recorded with nobody else present. The fact find preferred delivery method is recorded as 'No Preference'. The risk profile report (category D) records no additional notes in any of its discussion sections (investor experience, capacity for loss, investment target, agreed risk level). The policy research document (category G) notes the client 'has a lot of pensions that he doesn't understand how they work' and that 'Current set up isn't working because it is too much information for him to understand'. The suitability report itself (category I) mentions a forward-looking Power of Attorney recommendation but records no current vulnerability assessment outcome, no support needs, and no third-party support. The ATR questionnaire responses (category D) show the client has 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares, EIS/VCTs, and pension planning/income in retirement. The extraction metadata explicitly states /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are NotPresentInDocument.

    Discrepancies:

      - The report records no vulnerability assessment outcome for either client, yet the file contains FG21/1 capability drivers: the client has stated 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares, EIS/VCTs, and pension planning; the policy research notes the client does not understand how his pensions work and finds multiple plans too much information to comprehend.

      - No support needs, communication adjustments or comprehension measures are recorded anywhere in the report despite the presence of low-capability indicators in the file.

      - No formal vulnerability assessment outcome is recorded anywhere in the case file (confirmed absent by extraction metadata for both /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome).

      - The report's Power of Attorney recommendation is future planning only and does not constitute evidence of a current vulnerability assessment or current third-party support arrangement.

      - The fact find records preferred delivery method as 'No Preference' with no further detail, and the report records no accessible format or communication adjustments despite the low-knowledge profile evidenced in the ATR questionnaire.

    The comparison can be made. The report is entirely silent on vulnerability assessment outcomes, support needs, communication preferences and third-party support. The extraction metadata confirms these fields are NotPresentInDocument rather than an extraction failure. Against this silence, the file contains clear FG21/1 low-capability drivers: the ATR questionnaire records 'No understanding / knowledge' across all three investment/pension categories; the policy research document records that the client does not understand how his pensions work and finds multiple plans cognitively overwhelming. These are textbook FG21/1 capability indicators that should have prompted a documented vulnerability screening and, if screened out, a recorded rationale. Neither exists. The Power of Attorney recommendation in the suitability report is distinguished by the guard as future planning, not evidence of a current assessment. The spouse/partner noted in the fact find was not present at the meeting (confirmed by the fact find: 'Anybody else present at the Meeting? No'), so no third-party support inference arises from that. The absence of any documented vulnerability assessment in a case with clear low-capability drivers meets the 'High' severity threshold under the guidance (no vulnerability assessment anywhere in a case with clear FG21/1 drivers). The outcome is Potential Concern.

  [G10.2] Communication preferences and formats

  Potential Concern — severity Low

    Report:   The canonical model holds no value at the path /vulnerability/perClient[]/communicationPreferences. The suitability report is silent on communication preferences and formats. The extraction confirms this path is absent from the document (not an extraction failure for this specific path, as the extraction's expectedButAbsent list does not cite this path — meaning the report simply contains no such content). The report does mention, in passing, that the ongoing adviser fee 'will be paid via the Aviva Platform pension scheme as you prefer to pay via your provider rather than directly', which is a payment preference rather than a communication preference. No accessible format requirements, language needs, literacy considerations, or adjustments to support comprehension are stated anywhere in the report.

    File:     The fact find (category B, [P9]) records a 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference'. The Aviva terms and conditions ([P24]) reference the client's 'preferred method of communication' in a general contractual context but do not record a specific preference for this client. The key features document ([P25]) states 'Please get in touch if you'd prefer this key features in large print, braille, or as audio' — a generic provider offer, not a recorded client need. No passage in the file records a specific communication format requirement, accessible format request, language need, or literacy adjustment for this client. The fact find records 'No Preference' for delivery method, which is a neutral finding rather than a positive indicator of need.

    Discrepancies:

      - The suitability report does not reference the fact find's recorded delivery method preference ('No Preference'), though this is a neutral value and its omission from the report causes no material inconsistency.

      - The vulnerability assessment outcome paths (/vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome) are both listed as NotPresentInDocument in the extraction's expectedButAbsent list, meaning no vulnerability or communication-needs assessment outcome was recorded in the report for either client — this is an absence rather than a contradiction, but it is a gap against FG21/1 requirements.

      - The suitability report contains no reference to whether accessible formats, language support, or comprehension adjustments were considered or offered, even as a nil-finding confirmation.

    The fact find records 'No Preference' for preferred delivery method ([P9]), which under the guard should be noted but not treated as evidence that a vulnerability or communication-needs assessment was conducted. The suitability report is entirely silent on communication preferences, accessible formats, and vulnerability assessment outcomes. The extraction confirms this is genuine report silence, not an extraction failure: the two vulnerability assessment outcome paths are explicitly listed as NotPresentInDocument. Under FG21/1, firms must consider vulnerability and communication needs for all retail clients, not only those with known drivers. The absence of any documented consideration — even a nil finding — means the report provides no evidence that this overlay was applied. The fact find's neutral 'No Preference' answer is consistent with no special need being identified, but it does not constitute a vulnerability assessment or a documented consideration of communication needs. The guard correctly prevents treating a standard contact preference as a vulnerability adjustment, but here the concern is the complete absence of any documented assessment, not a mismatch between a preference and an adjustment. The suitability report also recommends that the client set up a Power of Attorney ([P18]), which is a forward-looking prudential suggestion rather than evidence of a current third-party authority or support arrangement in place. No Power of Attorney, trusted contact, or representative is recorded as active. On balance, the case file and report together show that communication preferences were not positively asserted as a need, but equally no documented confirmation that vulnerability and communication needs were assessed and found nil is present anywhere. This is a low-severity gap: the neutral delivery preference and absence of any positive indicators reduce the risk, but the complete absence of a documented nil-finding assessment is a process gap against FG21/1.

  [G10.3] Adjustments made to support client comprehension

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The canonical model holds no value at the vulnerability/perClient[]/adjustmentsMade[] or vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[] paths. The extraction confirms these paths are absent from the document (NotPresentInDocument). The suitability report is therefore silent on any adjustments made to support client comprehension, any vulnerability characteristics identified, and any communication or format preferences accommodated.

    File:     The fact find [P2] records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' and 'Accessible format requirement: No Requirement'. The risk profile report [P19][P23] notes sections on investor experience and capacity for loss with 'No additional notes were recorded'. The suitability report itself [P4] mentions Power of Attorney as a prudent future step but does not record any current vulnerability assessment, adjustment or support need. No passage in the file records a formal vulnerability assessment outcome, any identified characteristics under FG21/1 drivers, or any adjustments made to the advice process to support comprehension. The searches Q10.3.1 and Q10.3.4 returned nothing, confirming those data points are absent.

    Discrepancies:

      - The suitability report asserts no vulnerability assessment outcome and records no adjustments made to support comprehension; no passage in the file records a formal vulnerability assessment having been conducted or documented.

      - The risk profile report records no notes in the investor experience or capacity for loss discussion sections, indicating no vulnerability indicators were explored or recorded there either.

      - No characteristics (health, life event, low resilience, low capability) are recorded anywhere in the file despite the client being aged 69 (above state pension age), with limited investment experience confined to workplace pensions, and a household cash flow showing a monthly deficit of £288 [P8] — all of which are FG21/1 drivers that ordinarily require consideration.

      - The fact find records 'No Requirement' for accessible format and 'No Preference' for delivery method but contains no evidence that these were the product of an active vulnerability screening rather than default entries.

    The extraction confirms that /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are absent from the document because they are NotPresentInDocument, not because of extraction failure. The file corroborates this: no passage records a vulnerability assessment, its outcome, or any adjustments made to the advice process. Against this silence, the file contains several FG21/1 drivers that should have prompted active consideration: the primary client is 69 years old and at or past state pension age; his investment experience is described as 'limited to workplace pension arrangements' [P5], indicating low financial capability; and the household budget shows a monthly deficit of £288 [P8], a resilience indicator. The suitability report mentions Power of Attorney as a future prudent step [P4] but does not connect this to any current vulnerability concern and does not record any adjustment made for the present advice. The fact find entries of 'No Requirement' and 'No Preference' [P2][P16] could represent genuine absence of need, but there is no evidence they were reached through a structured vulnerability screening rather than left as defaults. The guard on cancellation rights is not engaged here. No guard covers the absence of a vulnerability assessment where FG21/1 drivers are present. The combination of: (a) no formal assessment recorded anywhere, (b) no adjustments documented, and (c) identifiable FG21/1 drivers in the file (age, low capability, low resilience) constitutes a material gap under PRIN 2A and FG21/1. This is a Potential Concern at High severity because there is no vulnerability assessment anywhere in a case carrying clear FG21/1 drivers.

  [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The suitability report does not contain an explicit vulnerability assessment section. The report infers two foreseeable harms: (1) investment values may fall if the agreed risk level 5 does not align with true risk appetite, mitigated by client confirmation of awareness and no day-to-day reliance on pension funds; (2) switching costs and critical yield — the new plan is 4.24% more expensive including adviser charges, requiring £4,672 outperformance in year one, mitigated by critical yield analysis and adviser belief that 4.8% p.a. growth is achievable. The report notes a Power of Attorney recommendation but no vulnerability characteristics are identified, no communication adjustments are described, and no formal vulnerability assessment outcome is recorded. The extraction confirms /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are both absent from the document (NotPresentInDocument). No /vulnerability/perClient[]/impactOnRecommendation is present.

    File:     The risk profile report (category D) records no additional notes in the investor experience section, the capacity for loss discussion section, the investment target section, or the agreed risk level section — all four note fields are blank. The fact find (category B) records 'No Requirement' for accessible format. The ATR questionnaire responses show: no understanding of stocks and shares, EIS/VCTs, or pension planning; no investment experience in unit trusts, stocks, EIS/VCTs or pension products in the last 5 years; only cash/savings accounts held independently. The capacity for loss questionnaire responses are truncated but the risk profile document records an agreed level of 5 (Balanced) overriding a natural score of 4 (Cautious Balanced). The policy research checklist (category G) records 'ATR/CFL - Fully completed with detailed notes: Y' but the actual risk profile document contradicts this — all notes fields are blank. No vulnerability screening document, no FG21/1 checklist, and no recorded assessment of vulnerability characteristics appear anywhere in the file. The report does recommend Power of Attorney be set up, acknowledging the client's age and potential future incapacity, but this is not framed as a response to an identified vulnerability.

    Discrepancies:

      - The report asserts no formal vulnerability assessment outcome; the extraction confirms /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome is NotPresentInDocument — no assessment outcome was recorded in the report or evidenced in any supporting document.

      - The policy research checklist records 'ATR/CFL - Fully completed with detailed notes: Y' but the risk profile document shows all four narrative notes sections are blank — no notes were recorded.

      - The client's knowledge and experience is consistently recorded as 'No understanding/knowledge' across stocks and shares, pension planning and EIS/VCTs, and no relevant investment experience in the last 5 years — this is a low-capability indicator under FG21/1 that is not reflected in the report as a vulnerability driver or trigger for adjusted communication.

      - The suitability report overrides the ATR questionnaire result from 4 to 5, a material upward risk override, without any recorded adviser notes justifying the override in the risk profile document — the absence of notes is a concern given the client's limited knowledge and experience profile.

      - No communication preference adjustments, accessible format requirements, or literacy considerations appear in the suitability report notwithstanding the low-knowledge profile evidenced in the risk questionnaire.

      - No vulnerability screening or FG21/1 assessment document appears anywhere in the case file.

      - The foreseeable harms in the report are inferred by extraction and not explicitly framed as a vulnerability/foreseeable harm assessment in the report itself.

    The FCA's FG21/1 identifies low financial capability and limited knowledge and experience as drivers that should trigger vulnerability consideration. The risk questionnaire responses record 'No understanding/knowledge' for stocks and shares, pension planning and EIS/VCTs, and no relevant investment experience — these are clear low-capability indicators. The report does not reflect these as vulnerability drivers, does not record any vulnerability assessment outcome, and does not describe any communication adjustments or support measures taken in response. The extraction confirms both /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are NotPresentInDocument, meaning the report is silent on the point rather than the extraction having failed. The policy research checklist's 'Y' entry for 'ATR/CFL - Fully completed with detailed notes' is contradicted by the actual risk profile document where all notes fields are blank — this is a discrepancy but does not rescue the position, since it is the notes themselves that matter. The age of the client (69) alone is not a vulnerability indicator per the guard, but combined with limited financial knowledge and experience it reinforces the need for a considered overlay. The report does recommend Power of Attorney, which acknowledges future incapacity risk, but this is presented as generic prudent planning rather than as a response to an identified vulnerability characteristic. The ATR override from 4 to 5 without any recorded justification notes, in the context of a client with no understanding of pension planning or stock market risk, represents a material concern: an already low-capability client has been placed at a higher risk level than the questionnaire indicated, and neither the override rationale nor the capability context is addressed in a vulnerability overlay. The foreseeable harms recorded in the canonical model are inferred by the extraction and are not explicitly framed as a vulnerability/foreseeable harm section in the report. No FG21/1 assessment document exists in the file. Overall, this is a case with clear FG21/1 low-capability drivers where no vulnerability assessment appears anywhere in the file and the report's reasoning does not engage with the identified characteristics — this meets the Potential Concern threshold at High severity.

  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The canonical model records hasVulnerability as 'NotAssessed' for both clients (C1 and C2). The fields for categories, characteristics and assessedOn are absent from the extracted model, and the extraction log confirms these paths were NotPresentInDocument for both clients. This means the suitability report contains no vulnerability assessment outcome, no recorded characteristics, and no record of when any assessment was conducted.

    File:     The fact find (P3, P4) records C1 as aged 70, with a date of birth of 07/06/1956, working as an HGV driver. The accessible format field in the fact find (P10) reads 'No Requirement'. The fact find records no vulnerability indicators, no support needs, and no third-party authority (P3, P4, P7, P10). The risk profile report (P13) records no additional notes in either the investor experience or capacity for loss questionnaire sections. The file notes (P1) address ATR and capacity for loss but contain no vulnerability assessment, no reference to vulnerability screening, and no mention of support needs or communication adjustments. The cashflow tool (P19) records two clients with illness and death events modelled at age 100, which are standard planning assumptions rather than vulnerability indicators. No Power of Attorney, trusted contact, or representative is referenced anywhere in the file. No communication preference beyond 'No Preference' for delivery method (P7) is recorded.

    Discrepancies:

      - The suitability report records vulnerability as 'NotAssessed' for both clients rather than recording the outcome of a formal vulnerability screening — no assessment outcome, categories or characteristics are present anywhere in the report or extraction.

      - The file contains no evidence that a vulnerability assessment was conducted at any stage of the advice process, meaning the 'NotAssessed' status in the report is neither corroborated nor contradicted by a completed assessment record.

      - C1 is aged 70 (DOB 07/06/1956, age recorded as 70 in fact find P3), which is an FG21/1 life-stage driver warranting explicit consideration of vulnerability, yet no screening or consideration is recorded in any document.

      - The file notes (P1) discuss ATR and capacity for loss at length but are entirely silent on vulnerability screening, FG21/1 considerations, or any duty-of-care overlay for a 70-year-old client.

      - No accessible format assessment beyond a 'No Requirement' tick-box (P10) is evidenced; no record of how comprehension or communication needs were explored is present in any document.

    The decision logic requires: (a) both absent — no issue; (b) file records a vulnerability the report never acknowledges — potential concern; (c) report asserts vulnerability with no file record — potential concern. Here, no vulnerability is positively recorded in either the report or the file. On a strict 'both absent' reading this would be no issue. However, the check runs as an overlay on all cases and FG21/1 imposes an affirmative obligation to consider and record vulnerability screening, not merely to record identified characteristics. C1 is 70 years old — an age that under FG21/1 constitutes a life-stage driver requiring explicit consideration. The fact find records no vulnerability screening section having been completed beyond a 'No Requirement' accessible format tick-box (P10). The file notes (P1) are detailed on ATR and capacity for loss but wholly silent on vulnerability. The risk profile report (P13) records no additional notes in either relevant section. There is no record anywhere of how, when, or by whom vulnerability was considered. The 'NotAssessed' status in the canonical model is therefore not a finding that vulnerability was considered and nothing found; it reflects that no assessment was conducted or recorded at all. For a 70-year-old client undergoing a consolidation of five pension plans — a significant financial transaction — the complete absence of any vulnerability screening record meets the 'High' severity threshold under the guidance: no vulnerability assessment anywhere in a case with a clear FG21/1 driver (age/life-stage). The guard that age alone is not a vulnerability characteristic is noted: this finding is not that age constitutes a vulnerability, but that the presence of a 70-year-old client is a recognised FG21/1 driver that requires the question to be asked and recorded, and there is no evidence it was.
